L1695: ## **Applicant's Conclusions**
L1696: - PK of fedratinib was adequately described by a two-compartment model with first-order absorption incorporating a lag time and first-order elimination.
- From 200 mg to 600 mg, the fedratinib AUC exposure increased proportionally with dose.
- Patients with PV had 1.87-fold larger V_2/F and 1.54-fold larger CL/F as compared to patients with MF.
- Dose and body weight are statistically significant covariates on V_2/F, but none of these covariates is clinically relevant requiring no dose adjustment.
- CL_{CR} is a statistically significant covariate on CL/F, and MF patients with mild and moderate renal impairment had 10% and 37% increase in fedratinib exposure, respectively.
- Age, gender, race and liver function were not statistically significant covariates affecting fedratinib PK exposure.
L1697: **Reviewer's Comments:** The Applicant's population PK model appears adequate to describe the observed concentration-time profile of fedratinib following the administration of fedratinib ranged from 100 to 800 mg QD in patients with primary MF, post PV MF, post ET MF, PV or ET. Therefore, the PK model is acceptable to generate post-hoc exposure metrics, e.g. AUC_{0-24,SS} and AUC_{C1-C6} of fedratinib for exposure-response analyses for efficacy and safety measurements. Based on population PK analysis, the terminal t_{1/2} of fedratinib was 114 hours and the mean Vd/F was 1770 L at steady state in patients with MF.
L1698: Covariate analysis revealed that the fedratinib exposure was not significantly altered by Age (20 years to 95 years), race (White, Black, Asians), sex, body weight (40 kg to 135 kg), mild [total bilirubin \le upper limit of normal (ULN) and AST &gt; ULN or total bilirubin 1 to 1.5 times ULN and any AST] or moderate (total bilirubin &gt; 1.5 to 3 times ULN and any AST) hepatic impairment, mild renal impairment (CL_{CR} \ge 60 mL/min to &lt;90 mL/min as estimated by Cockcroft-Gault equation), thus no dose adjustment is needed for the above-mentioned specific populations. In addition, per the Agency's request, the Applicant also evaluated the effect of the different formulations on the PK of fedratinib. The results showed that formulation was a significant covariate on V_2/F but not on CL/F. Patients receiving the initial formulation and formulation 1A1 had 15% higher and 23% lower V_2/F, respectively, compared with patients receiving formulation 1B1. However, the impact of formulation on the PK of fedratinib was considered to be not clinically meaningful, as there was no effect of formulation on AUC.
L1699: ## 19.5.7.2 Exposure-Response for Efficacy
L1700: The fixed dosing regimens of fedratinib 400 mg QD and 500 mg QD were used in the registrational trial EFC12153, while a titrated fedratinib dosing regimen was used in supportive trial ARD12181. Patients in study ARD12181 received an initial fedratinib dose of 400 mg/day on Cycle 1 Day 1,
L1701: and the fedratinib dose was titrated upwards in 100 mg/day increments up to maximum of 600 mg/day, after End of Cycles 2 and 4 based on a lack of adequate spleen response (\ge 50\% spleen size reduction by palpation compared with baseline) and no unacceptable drug toxicity. As the exposure-response (E-R) relationships would be confounded by the different dose regimens if using the pooled data from the two trials, the E-R analyses were conducted separately for each trial using different PK exposure metrics for the following efficacy endpoints, i.e., spleen volume response, spleen volume percent change, and total symptom score response.
L1702: ## **Trial EFC12153**
L1703: The E-R analyses showed a linear correlation between percent change in spleen volume at the End of Cycle 6 versus cumulative log-transformed AUC from Cycle 1 to Cycle 6 (\text{LogAUC}_{\text{C1-C6}}), indicating the spleen volume reduction increases as the fedratinib exposure increases (**Figure 23**). In addition, the logistic regression analyses also showed that fedratinib PK exposure was a statistically significant factor affecting SVR and TSSR (Table 90). The positive slopes of \text{LogAUC}_{\text{C1-C6}} suggested that the probability of spleen volume response increases as the fedratinib PK exposure increases.
L1704: **Figure 23:** Locally Weighted Scatterplot Smoothing and Fitting Curve from Linear Regression of Percent Change in Spleen Volume from Baseline at End of Cycle 6 vs \text{LogAUC}_{\text{C1-C6}} from Trial EFC12153
L1705: The blue line is the locally weighted scatterplot smoothing line. The red line is the fitting curve from linear regression model, and the grey shaded area represents the 95% confidence band from linear regression.
L1706: Source: Response to FDA Request for Information on 03/13/2019, Figure 1.
L1707: **Table 90:** Model Summary of Incidence of ≥35% SVR and TSSR versus PK Exposure from Trial EFC12153

Slope of LogAUCC1-C6	2.02	0.424	<.0001	1.26	2.93

L1710: ## **Trial ARD12181**
L1711: As the E-R analysis might be confounded by the dose titration algorithm in Trial ARD12181, predicted cumulated AUC from Cycle 1 to 2 (AUCC1-C2) before dose escalation was used as the exposure metric for the E-R analysis. The E-R analyses showed a flat E-R relationship between percent change in spleen volume at the End of Cycle 2 and LogAUCC1-C2 (**Figure 24**). In addition, the logistic regression analyses also showed that fedratinib PK exposure was not a statistically significant factor affecting SVR and TSSR (Table 91). However, the E-R relationships in Trial ARD12181 is not definitive due to: 1) narrow exposure range, 2) the response had not reached steady state at the End of Cycle 2; and 3) the use of palpation for measurement of spleen size in Cycles 1 and 2 is more subjective as opposed to MRI/CT in Trial EFC12153.

L1718: **Reviewer's Comments:** The applicant's exposure-response analyses for efficacy are acceptable. The slope was 2.02 (2% increase in \ge 35\% SVR for every unit of increase in log exposure) based on the linear model in Trial EFC12153. Based on the slope and the mean difference in exposure between the two doses, only a 0.1% additional increase in \ge 35\% SVR at EOC6 is predicted at 500 mg versus 400 mg. This is due to the considerable overlap in exposure observed between the two doses (Figure 2) due to higher incidence of dose modifications (51 %) due to AEs at the 500 mg dose in Trial EFC12153.
L1719: ## 19.5.7.3 Impact of CYP3A4 Genotype on Exposure-Efficacy Relationship in Study EFC12153
L1720: The reviewers conducted the following independent analysis to evaluate the impact of CYP3A4 genotype on fedratinib exposure and the exposure-efficacy relationship in patients with MF who were naïve to ruxolitinib treatment in study EFC12153. There were two single nucleotide polymorphisms (SNPs) in CYP3A4: RS2740574 and RS4986910, associated with fedratinib PK exposure.
L1721: For CYP3A4 variant RS2740574, 5/8 (62.5%) patients with genotype "A/G" or "G/G" and 71/100 (71%) patients with genotype "A/A" had data on fedratinib AUC and percentage change in spleen volume at Cycle 6. Patients with genotype "A/G" or "G/G" (n = 5) tended to have lower AUC_{C1-C6} (3317 \pm 1089 mg\cdoth/L vs. 5477 \pm 1989 mg\cdoth/L), and less percentage change in spleen volume at Cycle 6 (-34.6\% \pm 16.8\% vs. -37.5\% \pm 20.0\%), as compared to patients with genotype "A/A" (n = 71). The E-R relationships for percentage change in spleen volume at Cycle 6 in patients with CYP3A4 variant RS2740574 genotype "A/G" or "G/G" and patients with genotype "A/A" are presented in Figure 25. The difference in E-R relationship in these two subpopulations may be driven by patient ID= [REDACTED] (b)(6) with genotype "A/G", 14.3 of \log AUC_{C1-C6} and -40% change in SV at cycle 6. In addition, the E-R relationships are also confounded by the imbalance in demographics and disease status at baseline between two subpopulations (Table 92).
L1722: **Figure 25:** The Exposure-Response Relationships for Percentage Change in Spleen Volume at Cycle 6 in Patients with Different CYP3A4 Variant RS2740574 Genotypes in Study EFC12153
L1723: Source: Reviewer's analysis.
L1724: **Table 92:** Demographics and Disease Status at Baseline in Patients Different CYP3A4 Variant RS2740574 Genotypes in Study EFC12153.
L1725: CYP3A4 variant RS2740574
A/G or G/G (n=5)	A/A (n=71)

L1728: For CYP3A4 variant RS4986910, 2/2 (100%) patients with genotype "T/C" and 74/106 (70%) patients with genotype "T/T" had data on fedratinib AUC and percentage change in spleen volume at Cycle 6. patients with genotype T/C (n=2) seems to have higher AUCC1-C6 (8613 ± 7125 mg·h/L vs. 5246 ± 1778 mg·h/L), and slightly greater percentage change in spleen volume at Cycle 6 (-41.5% ± 36.1% vs. -37.2% ± 19.6%) as compared to patients with genotype T/T (n = 74).
L1729: However, the results should be interpreted with caution due to the limited patients with genotype T/C in CYP3A4 variant RS4986910 and other potential confounders. In addition, a definitive E-R relationship cannot be established for patients with genotype T/C due to the limited sample size (**Figure 26**).
L1730: **Figure 26:** The Exposure-Response Relationship for Percentage Change in Spleen Volume at Cycle 6 in Patients with Different Genotype in CYP3A4 Variant RS4986910 in Study EFC12153
L1731: Source: Reviewer's analysis.
L1732: Overall, dose adjustment is not recommended for patients with genotype "A/G" or "G/G" in CYP3A4 variant RS2740574 or patients with genotype "T/C" in CYP3A4 variant RS4986910, given the lack of informative fedratinib PK exposure and definitive exposure-response relationships in these specific populations.
L1733: ## 19.5.7.4 Dose/Exposure-Response for Safety
L1734: ## *Dose-Response for Safety*
L1735: Based on safety pool datasets from Trials EFC12153, ARD12181, and ARD11936, dose-response analyses were conducted for the following safety measurements, TEAEs at Grade \ge 3, treatment-emergent SAEs, TEAEs Leading to permanent treatment discontinuation, TEAEs leading to dose reduction, TEAEs leading to dose interruption, anemia at Grade \ge 3, and thrombocytopenia at Grade \ge 3, using actual dose levels when the adverse events occurred. In general, the odds of experiencing an event in any of the 7 categories of safety measurements were higher at 500 mg than 400 mg (**Table 93**), which support the starting dose of 400 mg QD from a safety perspective.
L1736: **Table 93:** Odds Ratio Estimates and Confidence Intervals of Safety measurements at 500 mg vs. 400 mg
L1737: Estimate	95% Confidence Interval
TEAEs at Grade \ge 3	2.67	1.26 - 5.64
Treatment-Emergent SAEs	3.49	1.85 - 6.58
TEAEs Leading to Permanent Treatment Discontinuation	3.65	1.82 - 7.32
TEAEs Leading to Dose Reduction	2.47	1.48 - 4.12
TEAEs Leading to Dose Interruption	3.04	1.69 - 5.46
Anemia at Grade \ge 3	1.58	0.91 - 2.73
Thrombocytopenia at Grade \ge 3	1.50	0.73 - 3.05
L1738: Source: Response to FDA Information Request on 3/13/2019, Tables 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12 and 13.
L1739: ## *Exposure-Response for Safety*
L1740: A total of 404 patients from four Phase 2 studies (ARD12181, ARD11936, ARD12042 and ARD12888) and one Phase 3 study (EFC12153), who had both post-hoc estimated PK exposures (AUC_{0-24,ss}) from the final population PK model and safety measurements were included in the exposure-safety analysis. The AUC_{0-24,ss} at the nominal starting dose was selected for exposure-safety analysis because: 1) 73% of the patients experienced dose reduction or interruption while only 11% patients experienced dose escalations from the nominal starting dose, thus the AUC_{0-24,ss} at the starting dose generally represented the maximal daily exposure at steady state; and 2) other PK exposure parameters, e.g., C_{max,ss} and C_{trough,ss} of the nominal starting dose were highly correlated with AUC_{0-24,ss}. Applicant's logistic regression of exposure-safety analyses suggested that the increasing fedratinib exposure increases probability of anemia at Grade \ge 3 (**Figure 27**), thrombocytopenia at Grade \ge 3 (**Figure 28**), diarrhea at any grade (**Figure 29**), nausea and vomiting at any grade (**Figure 30**), any TEAEs at Grade \ge 3 (**Figure 31**), treatment-emergent SAEs (**Figure 32**), TEAE Leading to permanent treatment discontinuation (**Figure 33**), and TEAE leading to dose reduction (**Figure 34**), except for TEAE leading to dose interruption, which is independent of the fedratinib steady-state AUC exposure. In addition, the baseline hemoglobin contributed to a higher incidence of anemia at Grade \ge 3 (**Figure 27**) and any TEAEs at Grade \ge 3 (**Figure 31**), while platelet count also contributed to a higher incidence of thrombocytopenia at Grade \ge 3 (**Figure 28**) and any TEAEs at Grade \ge 3 (**Figure 31**).
L1741: **Figure 27:** Logistic Model of Probability of Anemia of Grade 3 or Above Versus LogAUC0-24,ss.
L1742: logAUC0-24,ss = log-transformed area under the curve from Time 0 to 24 hours post dose at steady state
The red line is the model predicted probability and the two gray lines are the 95% confidence bands from logistic regression. The six symbols with horizontal error bars represent predicted probability at the observed means (\pm standard deviations) of LogAUC0-24,ss at different dose levels. Red triangles represent responders and blue triangles represent non-responders.
L1743: Source: FEDR-MPK-002, Exposure-Response Analysis Report, Figure 13.

The six symbols with horizontal error bars represent predicted probability at the observed means (\pm standard deviations) of \log AUC_{0-24,ss} at different dose levels. L1766: **Reviewer's Comments:** The applicant's dose/exposure-response analyses for safety measurements indicated the proposed dosing regimen of 400 mg QD is generally associated with lower risk of TEAEs compared to 500 mg QD. In addition, the reviewer also conducted independent E-R analysis to assess the relationship between fedratinib exposure and potential WE or encephalopathy, or Grade \ge 2 AEs related to WE. The results indicated that patients with WE or encephalopathy (n=4) tended to have higher AUC0-24,ss (68.5 \pm 37.2 mg\cdoth/L) than patients without WE or encephalopathy (44.1 \pm 37.9 mg\cdoth/L) when on fedratinib 500 mg QD (n = 107) (
L1767: Figure 35A). Similarly, patients with Grade \ge 2 AEs related to WE (n=16) tended to have slightly higher AUC0-24,ss (42.2 \pm 26.0 mg\cdoth/L) compared to those in patients without Grade \ge 2 AEs related to WE (33.1 \pm 24.4 mg\cdoth/L) when on fedratinib any dose (n = 388) (Figure 35B). But overall, the association was not clear between fedratinib exposure and potential WE or encephalopathy, or Grade \ge 2 AEs related to WE, given 1) the limited number of WE or encephalopathy cases, 2) largely overlapping range of exposure in patients with/without WE or Grade \ge 2 AEs related to WE, and 3) potential confounders which were imbalanced between the two subpopulations (Table 94).
L1768: **Figure 35:** Comparison of Fedratinib Exposure between (A) Patients with and without WE or Encephalopathy on Fedratinib 500 mg QD, and (B) Patients with and without Grade \ge 2 AEs Related to WE on Fedratinib Any Dose.
L1769: Source: Reviewer's analysis.

L1798: Flowchart illustrating the study design for JAKARTA.
The study population is N = 225, randomized (1:1:1) into three arms:
Fedratinib 400 mgb
Fedratinib 500 mgb
Placebob
The study is divided into a Treatment Perioda and a Thiamine Supplementation Periode.
Treatment Perioda:
Cycle 1 Through End of Cycle 6: All three arms receive their assigned treatment (Fedratinib 400 mg, Fedratinib 500 mg, or Placebo).
Cycle 7 to End of Treatment: The Fedratinib 400 mg and Fedratinib 500 mg arms undergo a crossover (1:1) to the other fedratinib dose arm (Fedratinib 500 mg and Fedratinib 400 mg, respectively). The Placebo arm continues receiving Placebo.
Thiamine Supplementation Periode:
Subjects receiving Fedratinib 400 mg or Fedratinib 500 mg receive Therapeutic Thiaminef.
Subjects receiving Placebo receive Supplemental Thiamineg.
An Early Termination of Studyd is indicated, which applies to all arms.
L1799: Scatter plot showing the linear regression of Percent Change in Spleen Size at Cycle 6 versus LogAUC(C1-C6) from Trial EFC12153. The x-axis is LogAUC(C1-C6) ranging from 14.5 to 16.5. The y-axis is % change in spleen size at Cycle 6 ranging from -100 to 50. Data points are colored by dose group: 400 mg (red) and 500 mg (blue/cyan). A red line represents the linear regression fit, and a grey shaded area represents the 95% confidence band.

The X-axis is 'No WE or Encephalopathy' and 'Yes'. The 'No' group shows a wide distribution of AUC values, centered around 40-60 mg·h/L. The 'Yes' group shows a cluster of points, mostly below 60 mg·h/L, with one outlier around 100 mg·h/L. Plot (B) compares patients with and without Grade >=2 AEs Related to WE on Fedratinib Any Dose. The 'No' group shows a wide distribution of AUC values, centered around 40-60 mg·h/L. The 'Yes' group shows a cluster of points, mostly below 60 mg·h/L, with one outlier around 100 mg·h/L. Both plots show a horizontal red line indicating a median or reference AUC value, which appears to be around 40-50 mg·h/L.

Fedratinib 400 mgb

The Thiamine Supplementation Periode includes subjects receiving either Therapeutic Thiaminef or Supplemental Thiamineg.

L1805: Study Design ARD12181 (JAKARTA2) Timeline:
The study timeline spans from Screening through C10D1, marked by visits at C1D1, C2D1, C3D1, C4D1, C5D1, C6D1, C7D1, C8D1, C9D1, and C10D1. MRI/CT scans are scheduled at C4D1 and C7D1.
Flexible Dosing:
Starting dose: 400mg
Upward titration: 500mg
Upward titration: 600mg
Dose adjustment criteria:
Upward titration< 50% reduction in spleen size by palpation, and
No safety indication for study drug dose interruption/reduction/discontinuation

Downward titrationSafety indication for study drug dose interruption/reduction/discontinuation

Dose range (oral, once daily): 200mg, 300mg, 400mg, 500mg and 600mg
EOT* (End of Treatment): Subjects who complete 6 cycles of the treatment and will not continue their treatment or discontinue the treatment early for any reasons will undergo EOT assessments within a week (allowable visit window) following the last dose of study drug.
Follow-up**:** Follow-up visit should be conducted 30 days after the last dose of study drug.
L1806: Figure 9 is a line graph showing Mean \pm SEM Hemoglobin (g/L) versus Visit for three treatment arms: Placebo, 400 mg, and 500 mg. The Y-axis ranges from 85 to 117 g/L. The X-axis shows visits including C1D1, C1D15, C2D1, C3D1, C4D1, C5D1, C6D1, C6EOC, C8D1, C9D1, C10D1, C11D1, and C12D1.
The Placebo arm (red line) shows a slight increase in hemoglobin levels over time, starting around 103 g/L and peaking around 113 g/L near C8D1.
The 400 mg arm (green line) shows a slight increase in hemoglobin levels over time, starting around 109 g/L and stabilizing around 97 g/L after C6EOC.
The 500 mg arm (blue line) shows a significant decrease in hemoglobin levels over time, starting around 105 g/L and dropping to a nadir around 90 g/L near C3D1, before partially recovering to around 93 g/L by C12D1.

L1809: Figure 12 is a line graph showing Mean (SD) Plasma Fedratinib Concentration (ng/mL) versus Nominal Time (h) for three doses (300 mg, 400 mg, and 500 mg) across three visits in Study ARD11936. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 6000 ng/mL, and the X-axis shows time points: Cycle 1 Day 2 (0 hr), Cycle 1 Day 15 (0 hr), Cycle 2 Day 1 (0 hr), Cycle 2 Day 2 (0 hr), and Cycle 3 Day 1 (0 hr). The 500 mg dose consistently shows the highest trough concentrations, peaking around 1500 ng/mL at Cycle 1 Day 15 (0 hr) and remaining high thereafter. The 300 mg dose peaks around 1500 ng/mL at Cycle 1 Day 15 (0 hr) and drops significantly by Cycle 2 Day 1 (0 hr). The 400 mg dose shows intermediate concentrations.
L1810: Figure 13 consists of two box plots comparing geometric mean AUC (top) and Cmax (bottom) versus Doses (mg) (5 to 600 mg) for single dose (left) and multiple dose (right) in patients with myelofibrosis. The AUC plots show AUC(0-24) (ng\cdothr/mL) on the Y-axis, ranging from 0 to 100,000. The single dose AUC relationship is AUC=Dose\cdot30, and the multiple dose AUC relationship is AUC=Dose\cdot80. The Cmax plots show Cmax (ng/mL) on the Y-axis, ranging from 0 to 8000. The single dose Cmax relationship is Cmax=Dose\cdot3.4, and the multiple dose Cmax relationship is Cmax=Dose\cdot5. Both AUC and Cmax values increase linearly with dose, and the multiple dose values are consistently higher than the single dose values.
L1811: Figure 14 displays box plots comparing dose normalized pharmacokinetic exposure (AUC and Cmax) for formulations Initial, 1A1, and 1B1 in healthy subjects (left column) and patients with myelofibrosis (right column). The plots are arranged in a 3x2 grid. The rows correspond to formulations: Initial (top), 1B1 (middle), and 1A1 (bottom). The columns correspond to exposure metrics: AUC (left) and Cmax (right). In healthy subjects (left), AUC values are generally higher than Cmax values for all formulations. In patients (right), AUC values are generally higher than Cmax values for all formulations. The box plots show the 25th and 75th percentiles, whiskers for min/max, median (black dot), and outliers (blue circles).

L1813: Bar chart showing the safety summary of hepatic impairment (HI) in fedratinib and placebo groups. The Y-axis represents % Patients (0 to 100). The X-axis shows three categories of HI: Normal, Mild HI, and Moderate HI. Within each HI category, two groups are compared: Placebo (n=62) and 400 mg (n=179) for Normal; Placebo (n=23) and 400 mg (n=75) for Mild HI; and Placebo (n=10) and 400 mg (n=17) for Moderate HI. The legend indicates six types of outcomes: Dose Int (Dose Intensity), Grade 3/4 TEAE (Treatment-Related Adverse Events), SAE (Serious Adverse Events), AE -> Death, AE -> TD (Treatment Discontinuation), AE -> DI (Dose Interruption), and AE -> DR (Dose Reduction). In Normal HI, Dose Int is highest for 400 mg (approx. 95%) and lowest for Placebo (approx. 30%). Grade 3/4 TEAE is highest for Placebo (approx. 65%) and lowest for 400 mg (approx. 10%). In Mild HI, Dose Int is highest for 400 mg (approx. 95%) and lowest for Placebo (approx. 30%). Grade 3/4 TEAE is highest for Placebo (approx. 60%) and lowest for 400 mg (approx. 10%). In Moderate HI, Dose Int is highest for 400 mg (approx. 95%) and lowest for Placebo (approx. 30%). Grade 3/4 TEAE is highest for Placebo (approx. 75%) and lowest for 400 mg (approx. 10%). SAEs are generally low across all groups and HI categories.

L1816: Figure 18 displays six Goodness-of-Fit (GoF) plots for the Final Population Pharmacokinetic Model for Fedratinib. The plots are arranged in a 3x2 grid:
Top Left: DV vs PRED. Shows observed data (DV) versus predicted values (PRED). The data points are scattered around the 45-degree line (blue), and the model fit (red line) is generally close to the observed data.
Top Right: DV vs IPRED. Shows observed data (DV) versus individual predicted values (IPRED). The data points are scattered around the 45-degree line (blue), and the model fit (red line) is generally close to the observed data.
Middle Left: CWRES vs PRED. Shows Conditional Weighted Residuals (CWRES) versus PRED. The residuals are centered around zero (red line), indicating a good fit.
Middle Right: CWRES vs TAD. Shows Conditional Weighted Residuals (CWRES) versus Time After Dose (TAD). The residuals are centered around zero (red line), indicating a good fit.
Bottom Left: CWRES vs TIME. Shows Conditional Weighted Residuals (CWRES) versus TIME. The residuals are centered around zero (red line), indicating a good fit.
L1817: Prediction-Corrected Visual Predictive Check (VPC) plot for the time profiles of Fedratinib. The Y-axis is Plasma fedratinib concentration (log, predcorr) ranging from 2 to 10. The X-axis is Time after dose (h) ranging from 0 to 25. Observed data points (blue dots) are scattered around the predicted median (red solid line) and the 90% prediction interval (blue shaded area). The plot shows the concentration profile over time, with the predicted median concentration generally decreasing after the initial peak.

L1826: Figure 27 displays two logistic regression plots showing the Probability of Anemia of Grade 3 or Above (Y-axis) versus LogAUC0-24,ss (X-axis). The red line represents the model predicted probability, and the two gray lines represent the 95% confidence bands.
The left plot is for Baseline Hemoglobin \le 10 g/dL, and the right plot is for Baseline Hemoglobin > 10 g/dL. Data points are shown for various dose levels (100 mg, 200 mg, 300 mg, 400 mg, 500 mg, 600 mg). Red triangles represent responders, and blue triangles represent non-responders. Horizontal error bars indicate the predicted probability at the observed means (\pm standard deviations) of LogAUC0-24,ss at different dose levels.
L1827: Figure 28 displays two logistic regression plots showing the Probability of thrombocytopenia of grade 3 or above (Y-axis) versus logAUC0-24,ss (X-axis). The red line represents the model predicted probability, and the two gray lines represent the 95% confidence bands.
The left plot is for Baseline platelet < 100 \times 10^9/L. The X-axis ranges from 9.5 to 11.0. Data points (symbols with horizontal error bars) are shown for 200 mg, 300 mg, 400 mg, and 500 mg dose levels. Red triangles represent responders, and blue triangles represent non-responders. The probability increases significantly with dose, especially for responders.
The right plot is for Baseline platelet \ge 100 \times 10^9/L. The X-axis ranges from 7 to 13. Data points are shown for 100 mg, 200 mg, 300 mg, 400 mg, 500 mg, and 600 mg dose levels. Red triangles represent responders, and blue triangles represent non-responders. The probability of thrombocytopenia is generally low across all dose levels for both responders and non-responders in this baseline group.
